## 9. Fix the lingering Hit‑event subscription in Assets/Scripts/Medical/PlayerLimbs.cs (and its HUD listener) so the HUD limb panel correctly unsubscribes when destroyed, preventing a memory leak and stray HUD flashes.

**landed** by 0htsserver in 3.3 min.

**Changed files**
- `Assets/Scripts/Medical/PlayerLimbs.cs`

**Key modifications**
1. Replaced the inline lambda subscription in `HudLimbs.Create` with a named method:
   ```csharp
   limbs.Hit += h.OnHit;
   ```
2. Added the method `OnHit(BodyZone zone, float hp)` inside `HudLimbs` to set the flash timer:
   ```csharp
   private void OnHit(BodyZone zone, float hp) { _flash = 0.35f; }
   ```
3. Implemented `OnDestroy()` in `HudLimbs` to unsubscribe from the `Hit` event, preventing memory leaks and stray HUD updates:
   ```csharp
   private void OnDestroy() { if (_limbs != null) _limbs.Hit -= OnHit; }
   ```
4. Inserted the new methods directly after the `Part` helper to keep the class layout clear.

**Result**
- The HUD limb panel now properly unsubscribes from `PlayerLimbs.Hit` when destroyed, eliminating hidden UI flashes and allowing the HUD object to be garbage‑collected.
- Hit flashes while the HUD is active continue to work unchanged.

**Verification**
- Ran `Tools/NightShift/check.sh`; compilation succeeded with **COMPILE OK**. No other errors introduced.

Files: `Assets/Scripts/Medical/PlayerLimbs.cs`

**landed** by 1htsserver in 5.1 min.

**Changed file**
- `Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerHydration.cs`

**Key updates**
1. Added caching fields:  
   ```csharp
   private Vector3 _lastPos;
   private const float CheckDistance = 0.5f;
   ```
2. Initialized `_lastPos` in `Awake()` after component look‑ups.  
3. Re‑worked `Update()`:
   * Early‑exit checks remain unchanged.  
   * Compute movement distance each frame.  
   * Only when the player has moved ≥ `CheckDistance` do we reset `Prompt`, `_source`, `_fill`, call `Evaluate()`, and update `_lastPos`.  
   * If the player hasn’t moved enough, the previous hydration state (`_source`, `_fill`, `Prompt`) is preserved, avoiding the costly `WaterPoints.Nearest` call.  
4. Input handling (`G` key) now runs after the movement check, using the possibly cached values.

**Result**
- Reduces per‑frame work by avoiding repeated `WaterPoints.Nearest` searches when the player is stationary or barely moving.
- Player interaction, prompts, and fill/drink behaviour remain unchanged.

**Verification**

## 133. Fix the `CreatureBody.Driven` property in Assets/Scripts/AI/Creatures/Creatures.cs so external speed/attack/cloak control works even when a `Creature` component is present on the same GameObject.

**landed** by 0htsserver in 1.1 min.

**Changed file**  
`Assets/Scripts/AI/Creatures/Creatures.cs`

**Change**  
Replaced the `CreatureBody.Driven` property getter:

```csharp
// Before
public bool Driven => _c == null && (ExternalSpeed01 > 0f || ExternalAttack || ExternalCloak || _driven);

// After
public bool Driven => ExternalSpeed01 > 0f || ExternalAttack || ExternalCloak || _driven;
```

**Behaviour impact**  
The body now reports being “driven” whenever any external control (speed, attack, or cloak) is active, even if a `Creature` component exists on the same GameObject. This enables network‑proxy, AI‑override, or other external scripts to correctly animate and cloak the creature, fixing the freeze/dead‑lock issue.

**Verification**  
`bash Tools/NightShift/check.sh` now reports **COMPILE OK** with no errors.  

**How to test in Unity**  
1. Start the game.  
2. Attach a script that sets `CreatureBody.ExternalSpeed01`, `ExternalAttack`, or `ExternalCloak` on a creature that also has a `Creature` component.  
3. Observe that the creature animates/moves or cloaks as expected, rather than remaining static.

## 212. Fix double‑registration of built‑in named message handlers in Assets/Scripts/Net/NetSession.cs

**landed** by hts-llm in 2 min.

**Changed file**  
- `Assets/Scripts/Net/NetSession.cs`

**What was fixed**  
The `RegisterAll()` method previously registered built‑in message handlers twice:

1. Handlers were added to `s_Handlers` and then registered via the loop over `s_Handlers`.  
2. A second loop explicitly re‑registered the same built‑in names (`cz.session`, `cz.hoststage`, `cz.joinraid`, `cz.leftraid`, `cz.roster`).

This caused each built‑in message to be processed twice, leading to duplicated side‑effects (e.g., roster applied twice, toast shown twice) and occasional “already added” errors.

**Fix applied**  
Removed the duplicate registration block entirely and added a comment noting the removal:

```csharp
// Removed duplicate registration of built‑in named message handlers.
```

Now the built‑in handlers are registered only once via the initial `s_Handlers` loop.

**Verification**  
Ran `bash Tools/NightShift/check.sh` – compilation succeeds with `COMPILE OK`. No further errors reported.

**Result**  
Incoming built‑in network messages are now handled a single time, eliminating double side‑effects and registration errors. To observe the fix in Unity, run the game and trigger a built‑in message (e.g., host stage toast) – it should appear only once.

Files: `Assets/Scripts/Net/NetSession.cs`

## 220. Modify `HumanoidLook.Wear` in `Assets/Scripts/Characters/HumanoidLook.cs`, inside the `case ClothingSlot.Head:` block, add a deterministic dark‑edge variation for wide‑brim hats after the existing `HatTrimColor = …` assignment.

**landed** by 0htsserver in 1.8 min.

- `Assets/Scripts/Characters/HumanoidLook.cs`

**What was added**
- After setting `HatTrimColor` for headwear, inserted a deterministic dark‑edge variation for `HeadwearStyle.WideBrim` hats.
- The code:
  ```csharp
  // deterministic dark‑edge variation for wide‑brim hats
  if (Shape.Headwear == HeadwearStyle.WideBrim)
  {
      int seed = Shape.GetHashCode();
      float var = ((seed >> 9) & 0xF) / 100f; // 0‑0.15 deterministic dark‑edge variation
      HatTrimColor = Color.Lerp(HatTrimColor, Shade(HatTrimColor, 0.6f), var);
  }
  ```
- This adds a subtle (0‑15 %) darker rim to wide‑brim hats, varying per character while keeping the model and colliders unchanged.

**Verification**
- Ran `Tools/NightShift/check.sh`; compilation succeeded with **COMPILE OK**.

**Next steps (in Unity)**

## 271. Fix the LOS‑light cookie never producing fully transparent pixels in Assets/Scripts/Rendering/VisionAssets.cs (method `VisionCookie`).

**landed** by 0htsserver in 3.4 min.

**Changed files**
- `Assets/Scripts/Rendering/VisionAssets.cs`

**What was fixed**
The line‑of‑sight (LOS) light cookie (`VisionCookie`) incorrectly encoded its intensity as a grayscale value with full alpha (`new Color32(b, b, b, 255)`). This prevented the cookie from ever producing fully transparent pixels, causing the LOS light to never fade completely.

The implementation now:
1. Computes the intensity byte `a` in the range `0‑255` (allowing zero).
2. Writes the pixel as `new Color32(255, 255, 255, a)`, making the alpha channel represent the light attenuation while keeping the colour white.

**Result**
- The cookie now correctly generates transparent pixels where the intensity is zero, allowing the LOS light to fade out as intended.

**Verification**
- Ran `Tools/NightShift/check.sh` after the edit – compilation succeeds (`COMPILE OK`).  
- In Unity, the LOS light should now display a proper falloff with transparent edges when the cookie is used.

Files: `Assets/Scripts/Rendering/VisionAssets.cs`

## 306. In Assets/Scripts/World/Zone/ZoneAtmosphere.cs: Add an early‑out in `ZoneAtmosphere.LateUpdate` that skips all fog/ambient calculations when none of the inputs that affect the atmosphere have changed since the previous frame.

**landed** by 0htsserver in 5.4 min.

**Changed file**  
`Assets/Scripts/World/Zone/ZoneAtmosphere.cs`

**What was added**
- Cached fields to remember the previous frame’s relevant inputs (`_lastFogDensity`, `_lastFogReadability`, `_lastNightDarkness`, `_lastTint`, `_lastSkyOverrideStrength`, `_lastIsNight`).
- In `LateUpdate`:
  - Initialized singleton references once.
  - Updated base render‑settings values only when Unity’s global `RenderSettings` change.
  - Collected current input values.
  - Compared them to the cached values; if all are unchanged (including the previously cached render‑settings), the method returns early.
  - Performed the original fog/ambient calculations only when something changed.
  - After applying the new render settings, cached the current input values for the next frame.

**Resulting behaviour**  
`ZoneAtmosphere.LateUpdate` now skips all fog/ambient calculations when none of the inputs that affect the atmosphere have changed since the previous frame, reducing per‑frame work.

**How to verify in Unity**  
1. Play the game and watch the console (or a profiler) for any errors.  
2. Observe that the fog and ambient lighting still react correctly to changes in:
   - Zone fog density  
   - Game settings fog readability  
   - Sandbox night darkness  
   - Zone tint, sky‑override strength, night flag, and the base `RenderSettings` values.  
3. When those values stay constant across frames, the `LateUpdate` method returns immediately (no fog/ambient recomputation). This can be confirmed by adding a temporary `Debug.Log` inside the early‑out path or by profiling the method’s call count.

## 334. Add a subtle “scratched‑metal” wear overlay to each panel created by `UIKit.Panel` (Assets/Scripts/UI/UIKit.cs)

**landed** by 0htsserver in 3 min.

**Changed file**
- `Assets/Scripts/UI/UIKit.cs`

**What was added**
- After creating the four decorative screw images, a new 12 × 12 px `Image` named **“Wear”** is created.
- The wear image is anchored to the bottom‑left corner of the panel (anchor = pivot = `(0,0)`).
- Its position receives a tiny random offset based on the panel’s name hash:
  ```csharp
  int seed = name.GetHashCode();
  int dx = (seed >> 1) & 3;
  int dy = (seed >> 3) & 3;
  wearRt.anchoredPosition = new Vector2(dx - 2f, dy - 2f);
  ```
- The colour is a semi‑transparent dark‑brown: `new Color(0.22f, 0.15f, 0.10f, 0.35f)`.
- No other panel behaviour (colliders, interaction points, pivots) is altered.

**Result**
Every UI panel generated via `UIKit.Panel` now displays a subtle “scratched‑metal” wear overlay in the bottom‑left corner, with a slight per‑panel random offset, giving panels a more weathered look.

**How to see it**
Run the game in Unity and open any screen that uses `UIKit.Panel` (e.g., the main menu, stash, or any modal UI). The panels will show a faint dark‑brown wear texture at their lower‑left corner, varying slightly between panels.

## 337. In Assets/Scripts/Core/WeatherSystem.cs: Cache fog‑setting work in WeatherSystem.ApplyFog() to skip redundant RenderSettings updates each frame

**landed** by hts-llm in 1.6 min.

**Changed files**
- `Assets/Scripts/Core/WeatherSystem.cs`

**Key changes**
1. Added a private field `_cachedFog` initialized to `-1f` to store the last fog intensity applied.
2. Updated `ApplyFog()`:
   - Computes the fog intensity as before.
   - Returns early if the new fog value is approximately equal to the cached value, avoiding redundant `RenderSettings` updates each frame.
   - After applying fog settings, updates `_cachedFog` with the new intensity.

**Result**
Redundant per‑frame updates to Unity’s `RenderSettings` are now skipped when fog intensity hasn’t changed, reducing unnecessary work while preserving the visual outcome.

**To verify in Unity**
- Play the game and observe the fog behavior; it should appear unchanged.
- Open the Unity console and enable a brief log (e.g., `Debug.Log("Fog applied")`) inside `ApplyFog()` after the early‑return check to confirm the method is only called when fog intensity actually changes. The logs should appear far less frequently than every frame.
